- **2026-06-10/11 · Claude Code (evening, pushed) · drained `docs/handoff-2026-06-10-dbsat-residuals-and-cron30s.md` residuals + the TetrisLblock verify-challenge follow-up.** Four commits to `main`; one worker deploy via wrangler. **Item 9 — pack-events-ingest re-wedge (TWO layers; `bee4da2` p25 + `ec307dc` p26, deployed via wrangler; deployed-vs-HEAD drift checked = was p24, no drift).** Layer 1 (`bee4da2`, p25): the `topshot_pack_purchases` cursor was deterministically wedged — a 1796-row `pack_purchases` `INSERT…ON CONFLICT` timed out every tick. Fix: all three writes (`pack_purchases`/`pack_rips`/`moment_acquisitions`) chunk at `WRITE_UPSERT_CHUNK_SIZE=400`; `ignoreDuplicates` keeps chunks idempotent + counts exact. Layer 2 (`ec307dc`, p26) — **necessary because after p25 the cursor STILL stayed frozen (21:24Z→01:12Z) with zero pipeline_runs after 23:39Z:** the deeper cause was the 06-10 `d198e68` "ack-early" change — `runIngest` ran under `ctx.waitUntil` after an instant 202, and Cloudflare kills a post-response waitUntil task on its own background budget, routinely BEFORE runIngest reached its end-of-run flush + cursor advance + log (green "843ms" cron ticks, silent wedge — exactly the handoff Item-9 addendum-3 diagnosis). Rolled back to running `runIngest` IN-REQUEST (`return await runIngest()`): the request handler completes even after the cron client times out at 30s, so the cursor + log always settle. `SOFT_BUDGET_MS` 25s→20s for headroom; an overrun completes server-side and only shows a cosmetic cron "timeout". This is the addendum-3 option 1. **Revert:** `wrangler rollback` to p24 / `git revert ec307dc bee4da2`. **TetrisLblock verify-challenge (`baf9802`, from `docs/handoff-2026-06-10-verify-challenge-followup.md`):** a verified beta user dead-ended — all 8 listing-challenge candidates rejected by the live GQL check (locked/listed) while wmc believed them free. Item 1: pool 8→24 (strict RPC `p_limit` + relaxed `.limit`; break-on-first-success keeps common-case cost flat) + relaxed fallback now skips wmc-known-locked (`.or("is_locked.is.null,is_locked.eq.false")`, mirrors the shipped picker RPC). Item 2: the `no_listable_target` branch now fires a forced wallet-backfill re-walk + returns a reassuring "refreshing, retry in ~10 min, or owner attestation" message; `CHALLENGE_TTL_MIN` 30→60. Item 3: Dapper-custodied wallets (no FCL address, known-issue 0) now show friendly "use the listing method below" copy in both `SignInWithDapper` and the dashboard `verifyViaLink`. **Revert:** `git revert baf9802`. **Item 7 + Item 8 (`631b1de`):** sentinel `isSaturationError()` routes the statement-timeout/connection-pool/abort error class on the Sales-Ingest / FMV-Freshness / Sniper-Feed checks to a warn-level INCONCLUSIVE ("db saturated") instead of paging CRITICAL (the 06-10 12:44Z Telegram CRITICAL was 4 parts timeout noise); genuine breaches (zero sales, stale FMV) still page. Dashboard `refresh()` now catches network-level fetch rejections (iOS WebKit "TypeError: Load failed", Sentry NEXTJS-1M/1K), retries once after 1.5s, then degrades to a friendly toast instead of an unhandled rejection. **Revert:** `git revert 631b1de`. **DEFERRED (blocked on tooling, NOT shipped) — render-id partial index (Item 3a/5 + focus item 10a):** `pinnacle-wmc-render-id`'s candidate read (`wmc WHERE collection_id=<pinnacle> AND render_id IS NULL`) seq-scans (the only render_id index is `WHERE render_id IS NOT NULL`). The fix must be `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS idx_wmc_pinnacle_render_id_null ON public.wallet_moments_cache (moment_id) WHERE collection_id = '7dd9dd11-e8b6-45c4-ac99-71331f959714' AND render_id IS NULL;` — but the Supabase MCP caps single statements (timeout) and wraps multi-statement calls in a transaction (CONCURRENTLY forbids it), and the DB was actively saturated (~22-28% fail/hr) so a blocking build was unsafe. **OPERATOR (low-load, no-cap psql):** drop any INVALID leftover first (CC already dropped its `idx_wmc_collection_render_id_null` attempt), then run the CONCURRENTLY statement above. Until it lands, the hourly candidate_read 8s-timeout is KNOWN (the d198e68 202-wrap means it logs the failure, not a dead 500).

- **2026-06-10 · Claude Code (daytime, pushed; commits `f41caf4` + `c55d394` + `838d89b`, deploy `dpl_2FktoZApVxvxn54ZbntyiLERLCCB`) · DBSAT root cause fixed: wmc full-rewrite storm + 202-wrap sweep + badge-taxonomy cache (from `docs/handoff-2026-06-10-dbsat-wmc-rewrite-storm.md`).** **Item 1 (the actual disease, `f41caf4`):** pg_stat_statements named the wallet-backfill `.upsert()` batch the #1 DB-time consumer (37,615 calls, 4.0s mean, pegged at the 8s cap) — every payload carried `last_seen_at=now()` + `fmv_usd=null` so EVERY conflicting row always differed → full row rewrite + every wmc index + the per-row `trg_normalize_tier_wmc` trigger, for ~1.58M rows every 6h wave, exhausting Micro IO for hours (the source of the cron-30s-timeout flood, the 06:55Z wallet-backfill 5xx, the badge-taxonomy 5xx). Fix: new SECDEF `upsert_wmc_batch(jsonb)` (migration **`audit_20260610_upsert_wmc_batch_change_detect`**, applied live via MCP) that the writer is authoritative for ONLY `edition_key`/`serial_number`/`last_seen_at` (immutable per `moment_id`); an unchanged row with `last_seen_at` <24h old is SKIPPED entirely (no rewrite/index/trigger). **Corrected the handoff's draft from direct schema inspection:** `tier`/`player_name`/`set_name` are filled NULL-only by the post-pass JOIN (`backfill_wmc_metadata_from_editions`, COALESCE) — including them in the UPDATE SET would clobber post-pass values AND defeat change-detection (stored-non-null IS DISTINCT FROM excluded-null is always TRUE), so they're excluded; `fmv_usd` dropped entirely (it was being wiped to null every wave then re-filled by wmc-fmv-populate — hidden churn, now stopped). All 4 call sites swapped in `lib/chains/flow/wallet-backfill-helpers.ts` (runIdOnly + AllDay/Pinnacle details + paginated). Functionally verified: insert→written:1, no-op skip→written:0, edition change→written:1. Grants service_role-only. 24h `last_seen_at` granularity is semantically identical to `run_weekly_db_maintenance`'s 14-day stale-prune. **Item 2 (`c55d394`):** 202+after() wrap across 11 sync cron routes (the 56ad4ff reconcile auto-disable class) — check-alerts, drain-fmv-cold-tail, offers-sweep, backfill-pack-rip-metadata, refresh-pack-grail-metrics-mv, backfill-pack-pull-source-rip-id, daily-portfolio-snapshot, backfill-pinnacle-catalog, refresh-error-triage, classify-acquisitions-multicollection, and apply-fmv-haircut (live-only; dry-run kept synchronous since it's an interactive preview). Added `log_pipeline_run` to the two routes that lacked one (daily-portfolio-snapshot, refresh-error-triage) since HTTP status is no longer the signal; bumped refresh-error-triage maxDuration 30→60; aliased the import to `scheduleAfter` in backfill-pinnacle-catalog (local var named `after`). **Item 3 (`838d89b`):** badge-taxonomy module-level TTL cache (1h) + negative-cache + serve-stale-on-error (kills the ~126-fails/5min 5xx spike class; repeat lookups never touch the DB on a wave). tsc clean (all changed files); corruption-guard clean. **Expected:** wmc upsert mean 4,000ms → low hundreds; instance-wide statement-timeout fails across unrelated pipelines drop sharply on the next 00/06/12/18Z waves; cron dashboard goes green. **Revert:** Item 1 `git revert f41caf4` + `DROP FUNCTION public.upsert_wmc_batch(jsonb);`; Item 2 `git revert c55d394`; Item 3 `git revert 838d89b`. **OPERATOR follow-ups:** (a) re-run the CI cadence-lint workflow (Item 4 — `extract-cadence.mjs` runs clean locally, exit 0; the GHA red is in the flow-CLI step or transient); (b) Supabase Micro→next-tier compute is a Trevor decision (the heterogeneous statement timeouts at 22 conns / 0 lock waits say load outgrew Micro even after Item 1). **WATCH:** the next 00/06/12/18Z wmc waves — confirm `pipeline_runs` for wallet-backfill stays clean, no `fmv_usd` wipe (wmc-fmv-populate write-rate should drop), and trust-health + cron dashboard stay green. (The handoff suggested a staged 1-site-first rollout; all 4 sites shipped together since the RPC is functionally verified and the sites are structurally identical — the wave-watch substitutes for the per-site soak.)

- **2026-06-08 · Cowork DB (live, via MCP) + Claude Code (`5880eeb`, pushed) · `pinnacle-sync` render-FMV sweep timeout fix (handoff-2026-06-08-pinnacle-render-fmv-recompute; Trevor approved minimal-fix over the handoff's batch+cron approach).** The 06-08 10:07Z `pinnacle-sync` tick logged `pinnacle_fmv_recalc_render_all: canceling statement due to statement timeout` (`duration_ms` 10035), leaving `pinnacle_catalog.fmv_computed_at` stuck at 06-07 10:07Z (~31h stale). **Root cause was NOT throughput** (the handoff's premise): the loop prices ~1,809 renders over ~12.7k sales in **~2s** (06-07 finished in 2.2s) — it was the route's `service_role` connection inheriting the `authenticator` **8s** `statement_timeout` (service_role `rolconfig` is null), which the 10:07 cron-rush slowed the normally-2s loop past. Verified post-apply: `proconfig` = `{search_path=public,pg_temp; statement_timeout=120s}`; EXECUTE = postgres+service_role only (no anon/auth leak; CREATE OR REPLACE preserved grants); re-ran clean (1,797 priced / 13 no_data). **(3) Route — `app/api/cron/pinnacle-sync/route.ts` `maxDuration` 30→60** as Vercel-layer headroom (baseline ~2s, DB now allows 120s). NOT shipped: the handoff's `pinnacle_fmv_recalc_render_batch(300)` fn, its dedicated cron, watchlist entry, and pinnacle-sync repoint — all unneeded once the existing sweep has its timeout. This also unblocks the legacy `pinnacle_fmv_snapshots` retirement (the staleness was the only blocker on that front). Revert: re-CREATE the prior `pinnacle_fmv_recalc_render_all()` body without the `SET statement_timeout TO '120s'` line + `git revert 5880eeb`.

- **2026-06-08 · Claude Code (daytime, pushed) · HybridCustody read-only wallet-verification path (known-issue #0).** Built the CC half of `docs/features/hybrid-custody-verify-path-2026-06-08.md`: a Dapper TS collector connects ANY Flow wallet via FCL account-proof (read-only, no tx signed) and verifies a DIFFERENT saved wallet W when W is on-chain HybridCustody-linked to the signed address A (or W==A). Removes the listing-challenge friction for the core audience (TSCR-style). **(1) DB — migration `add_hybrid_custody_link_verify_method` (applied live via MCP).** Added `'hybrid_custody_link'` to `verify_wallet_via_fcl`'s allowed-method IN-list (`('fcl_dapper','fcl_blocto','fcl_other','hybrid_custody_link')`); body otherwise unchanged. CREATE OR REPLACE resets grants → re-asserted **service_role-only** (REVOKE authenticated/anon/PUBLIC, GRANT service_role — NOT re-granting authenticated; that was the vector-ii bypass closed earlier today). Verified post-apply: EXECUTE = postgres+service_role only; `has_method`=true. Revert: re-run the prior body with the 3-value IN-list (`('fcl_dapper','fcl_blocto','fcl_other')`) + the same grant re-assert. **(2) Route — NEW `app/api/profile/verify-link/route.ts`.** Mirrors `app/api/auth/fcl-verify` (single-use 5-min nonce + `fcl.AppUtils.verifyAccountProof`) and `verify-challenge/check` (`requireUser` + 6/min in-memory rate-limit). `proof.address` (A) is the only proven identity; the link comes ONLY from `get_all_linked_addresses(A)` (on-chain `linked_accounts`), never from the request. Verifies W iff `W==A` OR `W ∈ linked(A)`, then calls `verify_wallet_via_fcl(...,'hybrid_custody_link')` (service_role) + awards `link_wallet`. **Spec correction:** dropped the spec's `saved_wallets` upsert — the real unique key is `(user_id, wallet_addr, collection_id)` (3 cols, `collection_id` NOT NULL/TopShot-default), so the spec's 2-col `onConflict` would fail and invent a phantom row; instead, mirror verify-challenge/check (W is already saved; the verify RPC UPDATEs existing rows; 0 rows → 409 `not_saved`). 403 `not_linked` on no link. **(3) UI — read-only option in the dashboard verify modal** (`VerifyByListingModal`, `app/dashboard/page.tsx`): "Verify via linked wallet (read-only)" button above the listing flow, copy "Read-only — we never ask you to sign a transaction"; reuses `configureFclAuth`+`fcl.authenticate` (SignInWithDapper flow), POSTs `{wallet_addr:W, accountProof}` to `/api/profile/verify-link`; on 403 not_linked shows a one-line "list a Moment below instead ↓" fallback. tsc clean (route + dashboard). Security invariants held (the gate hardened earlier today): proof-only identity, link from on-chain events only, `verify_wallet_via_fcl` stays SECDEF service_role-only, user_id `requireUser()`-resolved. Revert: `git revert <commit>` (route + dashboard) + the DB revert above.

- **2026-06-08 · Cowork (DB, live) + Claude Code (daytime, pushed) · verified-wallet sybil-guard hardening (SECURITY).** Found by the HybridCustody-spec verification pass: the verified-wallet gate that protects `requires_verified_wallet` redemptions (`redeem_shop_item` checks `EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM saved_wallets WHERE user_id=p_user_id AND verified_at IS NOT NULL)`) was bypassable by any authenticated user with zero proof. The CLAUDE.md "no user-writable points path" invariant still held (points aren't writable) — but the verification gate in front of redemptions was not. Three vectors; all three now closed. (1) **DB (Cowork, live) — migration `audit_20260608_close_verified_wallet_selfwrite_holes`.** Vector (ii): `verify_wallet_via_fcl(uuid,text,text)` had `authenticated:EXECUTE` (the SECDEF default-grant footgun) and only guards `auth.uid()=p_user_id` — no proof inside — so any logged-in user could PostgREST-call it to self-verify any saved wallet; EXECUTE revoked from authenticated/anon/PUBLIC (sole caller is `app/api/auth/fcl-verify` via supabaseAdmin/service_role, AFTER `verifyAccountProof`). Vector (i): `saved_wallets` had GRANT ALL to anon+authenticated and an own-row UPDATE RLS policy with no column lock, so an authenticated user could INSERT any wallet_addr then `UPDATE saved_wallets SET verified_at=now()` to bypass proof entirely (authenticated TRUNCATE was an extra latent cross-tenant wipe risk, not RLS-gated); INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE revoked from anon+authenticated (all legit writes go through service_role server routes; SELECT kept, RLS-gated to own rows). **CC re-verified post-apply: `verify_wallet_via_fcl` EXECUTE = postgres+service_role only; `saved_wallets` anon/authenticated = SELECT/REFERENCES/TRIGGER only.** (2) **Route (Claude Code, this commit) — vector (i)/route.** `app/api/auth/fcl-verify/route.ts` ran `verifyAccountProof` correctly (single-use 5-min nonce via `app/api/auth/fcl-nonce` + `fcl.AppUtils.verifyAccountProof`) but the proof proves control of `proof.address`, while the wallet actually marked verified was the SEPARATE top-level `body.addr` (passed to `verify_wallet_via_fcl` as `p_wallet_addr`) — a malicious client could submit a valid proof for a wallet it owns while posting a whale's addr and get that whale verified. Added, after `valid`: assert `provenAddr (=proof.address.toLowerCase()) === addr` else 401 "Address does not match the signed account proof"; type widened to include `address?: string`. tsc clean on the route. Defense-in-depth left documented (not needed now that write grants are revoked): if a future migration re-GRANTs `authenticated` UPDATE on `saved_wallets`, add a BEFORE UPDATE trigger freezing `verified_at`/`verification_method` unless `current_user='service_role'`, or a column-level grant excluding those two columns. Reverts: (DB) `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.verify_wallet_via_fcl(uuid,text,text) TO authenticated; GRANT INSERT,UPDATE,DELETE,TRUNCATE ON public.saved_wallets TO anon,authenticated;` (route) `git revert <this commit>`. Detail: `docs/handoff-2026-06-08-verified-wallet-security.md`. Note for the future HybridCustody build (`docs/features/hybrid-custody-verify-path-2026-06-08.md`): `verify_wallet_via_fcl` rejects unknown methods (`IF p_method NOT IN ('fcl_dapper','fcl_blocto','fcl_other') THEN RAISE`) — adding `'hybrid_custody_link'` is one line, and CREATE OR REPLACE resets grants → re-assert service_role-only (NOT authenticated — that's vector ii).

- **2026-06-08 · Cowork (DB, live via MCP) · `audit_20260608_revoke_inert_writes_sensitive_tables` (defense-in-depth; sibling of the verified-wallet hardening above, recorded late by CC from `docs/handoff-2026-06-09-session-closeout.md`).** Revoked `INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE` from `anon, authenticated` on 8 security-sensitive tables that are already service_role-only by RLS policy — the grants were inert, so this just removes them so a future policy drift can't expose them. Tables: `pro_users`, `pro_payment_log`, `stripe_payment_log`, `feature_quotas`, `deny_list`, `seeded_wallets`, `mcp_api_keys`, `linked_accounts` (the last is the HybridCustody verify path's source of truth — most important to keep locked). Extends the `saved_wallets` revoke (`audit_20260608_close_verified_wallet_selfwrite_holes`) and the backup-table lock (`audit_20260609_lock_pinnacle_backup_and_rewards_tier_search_path`) to the rest of the escalation-relevant tables. Revert: `GRANT INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE ON public.pro_users, public.pro_payment_log, public.stripe_payment_log, public.feature_quotas, public.deny_list, public.seeded_wallets, public.mcp_api_keys, public.linked_accounts TO anon, authenticated;`

## 2026-06-03 FMV sweep F-items — SHIPPED by Claude Code (Trevor-directed, preview-first)

Full drafts: `docs/handoff-2026-06-03-fmv-sweep-drafts.md`. Each change was preview-verified live before applying; no FMV snapshot was live-patched.

- **F3 · SHIPPED (code, fmv-recalc) · Defensive serial>circ recalc guard.** New Step 2a-ter in `app/api/fmv-recalc/route.ts` drops sales whose `serial_number > circulation_count` from each edition before WAP (impossible serials = a mis-keyed mint block of a different moment). Protects the whole mis-key class regardless of attribution cleanup. Preview: 102 impossible sales / 26 editions in the live 30d window (1,865 / 51 all-time). Scoped to fmv-recalc only — `upsert_topshot_marketplace_fmv` prices off the GQL jsonb payload (no serial data, never reads `sales`), so it can't be poisoned this way. Revert: `git revert` the Task-2 commit (or delete the Step 2a-ter block).
- **F4 · SHIPPED (migration + dashboard) · STALE excluded from headline portfolio total.** Migration `audit_20260603_wallet_collection_stats_split_stale` — DROP+CREATE `get_wallet_collection_stats(text)` adding a confidence-aware editions+latest-snapshot join: `fmv_total` now excludes STALE; new `fmv_stale_total` + `stale_count` surface it. Same STABLE/SECDEF/search_path/8s-timeout; grants re-locked to service_role+postgres (anon/authenticated explicitly revoked — default-privilege regrant on CREATE). Timing 2.21s on Trevor's 18,451-moment wallet (<8s). Live split: $5,425.24 STALE across 120 moments (66.5k excl-STALE TS total). Dashboard `app/dashboard/page.tsx` Portfolio FMV tile shows a footnote `+ $X across N stale-priced moments`. Revert: `git revert` Task-2 commit + re-CREATE the prior 9-column function body (in this session's transcript / prior `pg_get_functiondef`); re-grant service_role only.
- **F2 Tier A · SHIPPED (migration, DESTRUCTIVE-data) · 8:62 Cosmic→Clamps re-map.** Migration `audit_20260603_remap_cosmic_8_62_tier_a_to_clamps_226_7541` — moved the 22 sales + 16 moments wmc-confirmed (0 ambiguous keys, no fan-out) from Cosmic 8:62 (`5d122b9b-…`, circ 49) to De'Andre Hunter "Clamps" 226:7541 (`69389eea-…`). No FMV patched — fmv-recalc reprices both naturally. Post-state: 0 wmc-confirmed rows left mis-keyed; Clamps now has its 22 sales. **Revert (exact row ids captured this session):** `UPDATE sales SET edition_id='5d122b9b-2ee6-4cec-b64e-1b9315d17283' WHERE id IN (<22 sale ids in transcript>); UPDATE moments SET edition_id='5d122b9b-…' WHERE id IN (<16 moment ids>);`. **Tier B still open:** 65 residual serial>49 sales on Cosmic have no wmc row — need on-chain (V1/V2 Dapper) confirmation before moving. The F3 guard already excludes them from Cosmic's WAP in the meantime, so pricing is protected even pre-cleanup.
- **F5 · SHIPPED (code, fmv-recalc) · Step 5b NO_DATA recovery (corrected D3).** The "60→90d lookback" premise was wrong (no such window; grails have zero sales = ingest gap). Real lever shipped: `latest_algo` CTE now selects `confidence`; the Step 5b WHERE guard relaxed to `… OR la.confidence = 'NO_DATA'` (scoped to NO_DATA only — avoids the 2026-05-30 Step 6 re-clobber class); recovered rows tagged SALES_ONLY (`sales_count >= 5`) else STALE, leaving the pre-existing non-1.7.x population's LOW/STALE labels unchanged. Preview: 38 recoverable editions → 10 SALES_ONLY + 28 STALE. Did NOT change any lookback window and did NOT claim to fix the grails. Revert: `git revert` Task-2 commit.
- **F6 · NO ACTION (premise false, confirmed).** No double-discount: the haircut RPC is gated by `abs(fmv−floor)<0.01` and ASK_ONLY rows are written `fmv=ask×0.90, floor=raw ask` (fmv≠floor) so they're excluded. Forcing "consolidation" would drop 209 editions 0.90→0.55. Resolved-no-action.

- **F1 · investigation (data corruption, broader batch) — open.** The `serial > circulation` detector finds ~15 TS editions (≥5 impossible sales) total; F2 fixed 8:62, and F3 now defends the WAP for the whole class. The clean RARE/LEGENDARY sub-batch (127:4681, 127:4683, 29:907, 64:2375, 29:897 …) shares 8:62's single-target-wmc signature and could get the same Tier-A re-map; the messy/multi-key Base COMMONs + stale-circ false positives (Chet 245:8446) need per-edition analysis. Root cause to find: the moments-edition writer that mis-keyed contiguous mint blocks. **wmc is canonical over moments/sales for nft_id→edition.**

### 2026-06-16 (daytime CC) — underpriced-serials Item 2: edition map + GitHub-Actions curl ingest BUILT (public page still TODO)
Drained most of `docs/handoff-2026-06-16-underpriced-serials-ingest-build.md` (Trevor greenlit the build). Built: the RPC↔Atlas edition map, then — after the Vercel-egress block was proven — the ingest as a GitHub-Actions curl runner + thin Vercel DB route. NOT built yet: the public `/insights/underpriced-serials` page + the saved-subscription serial alert filters.
- **PROBE PASS — join key confirmed.** `editions.set_id_onchain == Atlas.setId` AND `editions.play_id_onchain == Atlas.editionTemplateId`, verified on edition `26:695` / Atlas `2017` across set+play+player(LeBron/PlayerId 2544)+circ(35000)+game_date(2021-01-11). So `external_id` (`setID:playID`) maps cleanly to Atlas's opaque integer `editionId`.
- **SHIPPED — migration `audit_20260616_topshot_atlas_edition_map`.** Table `topshot_atlas_edition_map` (rpc_edition_id PK → editions, external_id, set/play_id_onchain, atlas_edition_id, num_minted, tier, mapped_at; RLS on, service-role-only) + service-role `upsert_topshot_atlas_edition_map(jsonb)` that joins raw Atlas rows to editions DB-side. Security clean (`check_public_security_invariants`=0, anon EXECUTE denied). **Revert:** `DROP FUNCTION public.upsert_topshot_atlas_edition_map(jsonb); DROP TABLE public.topshot_atlas_edition_map;`.
- **SHIPPED — `scripts/backfill-atlas-edition-map.mjs` (committed) + run.** Reads RPC's 250 distinct TS sets, enumerates each via Atlas `EditionService/SearchEditions` (`setId:["N"]` filter, stable per-set pagination, ~402 gentle curl calls) → upsert RPC. Coverage: **9,091/9,137** canonical editions, **100% of board candidates** (`topshot_serial_board_candidates`: 2,892/2,892, 0 unmapped ≥$100). Residual 46 unmapped = non-candidate special/parallel sets (Honors Diced #152, Fit Check #102, Anthology/Signature) where RPC's set_id_onchain has no Atlas set (`totalCount 0`).
- **⚠ BLOCKING FINDING for the ingest route — Atlas WAF 403s Node/undici `fetch`.** Same IP + same headers (Origin/Referer/UA + 2 Connect headers): `curl` → 200, Node `fetch` → 403 "block" page. TLS/HTTP fingerprint, not IP/headers. **VERIFIED on Vercel (2026-06-16, throwaway `/api/admin/atlas-egress-probe`, since removed):** a real Vercel function (iad1) got 403 on BOTH minimal and enriched (Accept/sec-fetch-*) headers — so the handoff's "egress CONFIRMED from Vercel, no Worker needed" is WRONG, and header enrichment doesn't help (TLS fingerprint). NOTE the inverse of Trevor's same-day AllDay finding (`5f1a28d`: nflallday WAF 403s Cloudflare Workers/edge but PASSES Vercel) — so a Cloudflare Worker proxy is NOT guaranteed to pass Atlas either; it must be tested before relying on it. Only proven-good Atlas client = curl/browser (the map script). **Ingest Atlas-access options for Trevor:** (A) Cloudflare Worker proxy — but TEST a Worker→Atlas fetch first (may 403 like AllDay); (B) GitHub-Actions cron running a curl-based Node ingest script directly to Supabase — PROVEN client (the map run), repo already runs GH-Actions crons, no Vercel route needed for the fetch; (C) undici TLS-fingerprint workaround on Vercel — fragile, not recommended. **Trevor chose (B).** Memory: `atlas-undici-403-and-edition-map`.
- **BUILT + VALIDATED — GitHub-Actions curl ingest (`489a04b` route+runner, `998b8a3` workflow via gh contents API — the PAT lacks `workflow` scope).** Three new pieces: (1) migration `audit_20260616_topshot_serial_board_targets` then `…_targets_jsonb` — service-role `topshot_serial_board_targets(min_no1_estimate)` = board candidates INNER JOIN the Atlas map. **Returns `jsonb` not `TABLE`** (a `TABLE` return is PostgREST-row-capped at 1000 → it silently dropped 80 of 1,080 targets; jsonb is uncapped — caught in validation). (2) Vercel route `/api/cron/topshot-active-listings-ingest` (INGEST/CRON Bearer) — pure DB-I/O: `GET ?phase=targets&floor=N` → targets; `POST {rows}` → `upsert_topshot_active_listings`; `POST {deactivate:true,startedAt,stats}` → `deactivate_stale_topshot_active_listings('6 hours')` + `log_pipeline_run('topshot-active-listings-ingest')`. The route NEVER touches Atlas (service key stays on Vercel). (3) `scripts/ingest-topshot-active-listings.mjs` + workflow `.github/workflows/topshot-active-listings-ingest.yml` (every 3h @:13 + workflow_dispatch floor/max_targets/dry_run). The RUNNER curls Atlas `SearchMarketplaceTransactions(completed:false)` per target — SERIAL_NUMBER ASC limit1 (#1 end) + DESC limit1 (perfect end), accept only if serial==1 / ==circulation_count — and POSTs matched rows to the route. **No new GH secret** (reuses `INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN`). `listing_url` left NULL — dapper.market's per-serial deep-link format is undiscoverable (the site WAF-blocks curl too); board drill-down uses `nft_id`→`/moment/<id>` (TODO: confirm the dapper deep-link). The whole chain was **VALIDATED end-to-end from a residential IP** (40-target real run: 9 boundary listings written, `pipeline_runs` ok=true, board surfaced 1 real deal — Azzi Fudd WNBA Rookie Debut #1/1149, $1500 ask vs $1519 est).
- **⛔ BLOCKER — the GitHub-Actions runner is ALSO WAF-blocked by Atlas (datacenter IP).** A dry-run on the actual GH runner (run 27671918131) got `<title>block</title>` on EVERY curl (5/5 skipped, 0 successful). The earlier block page even cited "geo-location and IP address" → **Atlas blocks datacenter IPs regardless of client (curl OR undici)**. So: residential IP (my box) = PASS; Vercel = BLOCK; GH runner = BLOCK. The "GitHub-Actions + curl" path does NOT clear the WAF. **Workflow DISABLED** (`gh workflow disable`) so it can't run uselessly/harmfully. Hardened the runner+route so a fully-blocked run logs a failure and NEVER deactivates (deactivate decoupled from logging via a `final` flag) — commit `<pending>`. **The build is complete and correct; the only missing piece was a non-datacenter egress.** **RESOLVED — Trevor chose to run it on his home machine.** Shipped `scripts/run-active-listings-ingest.ps1` (Task Scheduler wrapper: loads INGEST/CRON token from `.env.local`, runs the Node runner, logs to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\rpc-deal-board-ingest\ingest.log`) + `scripts/register-active-listings-task.ps1` (idempotent registrar). **Scheduled task `RPC Deal Board Ingest` REGISTERED on Trevor's machine — every 3h @:13, runs while logged on.** Remove: `Unregister-ScheduledTask -TaskName "RPC Deal Board Ingest" -Confirm:$false`. **Revert (full):** unregister the task + delete the workflow + scripts + route + `DROP FUNCTION public.topshot_serial_board_targets(numeric);`.

### 2026-06-14 (daytime CC) — Q8 badge-sync grain: residue cleaned + integer-pair grain made durable
`audit_20260614_badge_editions_topshot_intpair_grain_guard`. **Q8 root cause (the `onConflict:"id"` vs `UNIQUE(external_id,collection_id)` mismatch that poisoned ~40% of batches) was already fixed in `0a8c5db`/`5bd3cd6`** — the route now upserts `onConflict:"external_id,collection_id"` (matches the constraint exactly) and dedups to one row per canonical on-chain integer pair `setID:playID` (== `editions.external_id`, what `get_edition_badges_unified` joins on) via the per-key Map + `mergeTags` + `keyedIds` pre-delete. Verified resolved: live `topshot-badge-catalog` runs show `upsert_errors:0` across all recent ticks; table was 8939 rows / 0 nulls / 0 duplicates. **Correct grain = one row per canonical integer-pair edition** (each TS parallel is its own `setID:playID`, so per-parallel == per-edition-external_id; badges are play-level and union across parallels into each edition's row). This migration cleans the pre-fix residue + makes the grain durable: (1) DELETEd the **2 surviving UUID-keyed fossils** (Jalen Brunson "Run It Back: Origins", SGA "2025 NBA Playoffs: Ultimate" — written by the pre-`0a8c5db` UUID-pair keyer, no integer-keyed counterpart, can NEVER join the canonical edition → phantom data); (2) added trigger `badge_editions_block_topshot_uuid_key_trg` (mirrors `editions_block_topshot_uuid_dupe_trg`) that silently drops (`RETURN NULL`, batch-safe) any TS badge row whose `external_id` isn't `^[0-9]+:[0-9]+$`, so a future writer regression can't re-poison the table — scoped to TS only (AllDay/Golazos badges use other key formats and pass through). Verified: table now 8937 / 0 nulls / 0 non-int-pair / 0 dupes; trigger drops a UUID-keyed TS insert with no error (0 landed) and lets an int-pair insert through (1 landed); test rows cleaned. **Revert:** `DROP TRIGGER badge_editions_block_topshot_uuid_key_trg ON public.badge_editions; DROP FUNCTION public.badge_editions_block_topshot_uuid_key();` (the 2 deleted fossil rows were unreachable — no restore needed; they re-create as proper int-keyed rows if/when their set enters the sets-map). **Target metric:** badge_editions TS rows stay 100% int-pair-keyed; `topshot-badge-catalog` `upsert_errors` stays 0.

### 2026-06-14 (daytime CC) — OFFER-SANITY-RAISE: edition-grain filter hardened + SECDEF anon hole closed
`audit_20260614_offer_sanity_raise_edition_grain_filter` — `raise_edition_offers_from_chain()` (the GREATEST raise wired into the `offers-sweep` cron every ~20m) rewritten so only **edition-grain** on-chain offers can set the edition Best Offer cell: filter changed `offer_type = 'edition'` → `offer_type NOT IN ('subedition','serial')` (defensive form — includes any future edition-grain type, still excludes parallel/serial offers which live at a finer grain). Recurring cron CONFIRMED: `offers-sweep` calls this RPC each tick and is in `pipeline_cadence_watchlist` (active, 120m). Pre-fix diagnostic: 0 editions where an *edition*-type chain offer exceeded the displayed value (the prior `='edition'` raise was already catching them); the change is a defensive/no-regression hardening + the security fix. **Revert:** `CREATE OR REPLACE` the prior body (`WHERE ... offer_type = 'edition'`) and `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.raise_edition_offers_from_chain() TO anon, authenticated;` (don't — leave revoked). **Target metric:** `offers-sweep` `extra->>'offers_raised_from_chain'` stays >0 each tick; no sub/serial value ever appears as an edition Best Offer.

### 2026-06-13 (Vercel cost reduction — CC) — SHIPPED

- **`0e7e627` — skip prod build on docs-only commits (`vercel.json` `ignoreCommand`).** VERCEL-COST handoff Item 1, the biggest lever (Build CPU was ~55% of the Vercel overage, ~7 build-hours/day) because every push to `main` rebuilds prod — including the frequent docs-only commits from the daytime monitor / nightly pass / ledger+focus+handoff writes, each a full turbopack build for zero production change (the deploy list shows `3456e6e` docs(ledger), `6291bc16` docs, and the `monitor:` commits all built to READY). Added `"ignoreCommand": "git diff --quiet HEAD^ HEAD -- . ':(exclude)docs/**' ':(exclude)*.md' ':(exclude)*.mdx'"` — Vercel skips the build when the exit code is 0 (only `docs/**` + `*.md`/`*.mdx` changed), builds on exit 1 (any `app/`, `lib/`, `components/`, `scripts/`, `vercel.json`, `package.json`, etc. change). Mixed code+docs commits build. A missing `HEAD^` (shallow/first commit) errors non-zero → falls through to BUILD (safe). Validated against real history before shipping: docs(ledger) `3456e6e` → exit 0 SKIP; code commits `bd8e05c`/`b08eb23`/`f073ae0` → exit 1 BUILD. Pathspec exclude is global (git fnmatch without FNM_PATHNAME, so `*.md` matches at any depth). **Revert:** `git revert 0e7e627` (or delete the `ignoreCommand` key from `vercel.json`). **Target metric:** Build CPU minutes / day drops sharply (docs-only pushes show "Build skipped" in the Vercel deployments list); projected ~$85/mo → toward the $20 included credit. **Remaining (handoff Items 2/3, not shipped):** Item 2 right-size the high-frequency × long-duration cron/Fluid compute (wallet-backfill family, fmv-recalc memory/maxDuration; cron-job.org cadence) — operator + CC; Item 3 dial down Observability sampling/log volume — lower priority.

User-viewed wallets escaped because `ufc-wallet-scan` (collection page / wallet-search / profile-resolve) enriches **synchronously** on first view (UFC wallets are all ≤18 moments = single page) — exactly the 29 enriched. (2) `ufc-wallet-scan` enrich-pagination cursor reads `nextStart` (the enricher's real field) instead of `next`/`totalEnriched` — masked today since all UFC wallets are single-page, but would silently truncate any >100-moment wallet. tsc-clean (changed files), corruption-guard clean. **Revert:** `git revert b28a22f`. **Target metric:** UFC `wmc` NULL-`edition_key` count drops from 3,150 toward <100 over the next ~6–24h of seed-refresh cycles; verify with the sweep's check-3b query. If it does NOT drop within ~24h, the after() chain is being killed for a deeper reason (decouple enrichment into its own cron drain — operator).

### 2026-06-22 (night pass) — DURABLE fix for the special-serial-owners MV refresh timeout: force the hash-join plan (`audit_20260622_refresh_special_serial_owners_mv_force_hashjoin`)
`audit_20260622_refresh_special_serial_owners_mv_force_hashjoin` — `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.refresh_topshot_special_serial_owners_mv()` adding **`SET enable_nestloop TO 'off'`** (+ defensive `REVOKE EXECUTE … FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` / `GRANT EXECUTE … TO postgres, service_role`). All other attributes preserved verbatim: SECDEF, `search_path='public','pg_temp'`, `statement_timeout='200s'`, `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY public.topshot_special_serial_owners_mv`, and the `log_pipeline_run` self-log in both the success (ok=true) and `EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS` (ok=false) paths.
- **Why:** the 06-22 monitor caught pg_cron job `rpc-refresh-special-serial-owners-mv` FAILING its first tick (04:13Z, cancelled at exactly 120.08s = the session-default `statement_timeout`, which the fn proconfig `200s` cannot re-arm mid-command). The monitor's Fix-A ("drop CONCURRENTLY → ~30-60s") was **disproved by measurement**: the underlying view `topshot_special_serial_owners` is **itself 112,699 ms** (lock-free EXPLAIN ANALYZE) — a planner misestimate picking a Nested Loop over ~10,888 canonical TS editions, each a wmc index scan fetching ~138 non-special serials before filtering. Plain REFRESH would be ~114s = still marginal and would fail again at the 16:13Z daytime tick. Forcing the hash join (`enable_nestloop=off`; default work_mem suffices — the editions hash is 1.5 MB) collapses the query to **~3,500 ms (~30x)**, so CONCURRENTLY is **KEPT** (no read-blocking AccessExclusive lock) and the refresh runs ~34x under the 120s ceiling. Mechanism: planner GUCs are read at each statement's PLAN time (unlike `statement_timeout`'s one-time arming), so the fn-entry proconfig DOES govern the inner REFRESH's plan.
- **Verified (fresh no-context subagent — VERDICT PASS, all 6 checks):** def has the SET clause + all attrs preserved; ACL `{postgres=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres}`; `check_secdef_anon_execute_violations()` []; off-plan EXPLAIN = Hash Join ~3.1–4.1s (vs on-plan Nested Loop 112,699 ms); **force-ran the exact cron path twice → both `pipeline_runs` ok=true / logged_by=fn / duration_ms 3540 & 3516**; MV freshened 6,778→6,783 (1.7d staleness cleared). Result is plan-choice only (join algorithm), not a correctness change — off-plan returns the same 6,783 unique rows.
- **Target metric:** the **16:13Z 2026-06-22** pg_cron tick logs a `refresh-special-serial-owners-mv` `pipeline_runs` row `ok=true` (low-single-digit-thousands ms); `cron.job_run_details` status='succeeded'; MV stays fresh; `ts-backfill-drain-serial-fmv-watch` stays quiet.
- **Revert:** `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.refresh_topshot_special_serial_owners_mv()` with the **same body minus the `SET enable_nestloop TO 'off'` line** (the prior def is preserved in the 06-22 self-log Shipped entry below). No data revert needed.

### 2026-06-21 (daytime, Claude Code) — flagged the thin-data fake-deal residual on the TS deal board (FLAG-not-suppress; 10/523 TS deals caveated, alerts suppress them)
Drained `docs/handoff-2026-06-21-deal-board-thin-variance-fmv.md`. The parallel-conflation guard killed the *conflation-driven* fake deals; this closes the last residual of the **same user-facing symptom (inflated FMV → fake discount) from a different cause: thin, high-variance sales**. WAP/mean FMV overshoots the 90d median on editions with few, wide-ranging sales, so a near-median ask reads as a big "discount". 2 migrations + 4 app files; tsc clean; **security invariants 0, `check_secdef_anon_execute_violations()` [], `topshot_deals_vs_fmv` `security_invoker=on` preserved**. Trevor's product call honored: **FLAG (caveat), not suppress** — plus alert suppression (the part most worth suppressing).
- **Measured (live) first.** `topshot_deals_vs_fmv` = **520 deals; precise definition (FMV >1.5× 90d median AND <15 sales/90d) flags 10** (~2%, dominated by low-circ Metallic Gold LE parallels: Aaron Gordon 5:61 $55 vs $24 median = fake 51% "deal", Carmelo 5:50, Cam Whitmore, Bradley Beal, Kawhi…). **The handoff's cheap proxy (`MEDIUM AND sales_count_30d<15 AND discount_pct≥35`) is a poor match — flags 13 but agrees with the precise set on only 5/10**, so it was NOT used. Used the precise median definition instead, computed off the hot path.
- **Mechanism = mirror the conflation guard, but FLAG via LEFT JOIN instead of suppress via NOT EXISTS.** No per-row LATERAL median in the hot-path view (the handoff's explicit perf guardrail — it feeds page loads + `dispatch_due_deal_alerts`). `fmv_snapshots` has **no stored median**, so the precise definition needs a scan → put it in a periodic refresh.
- **Migration 1 — `audit_20260621_topshot_thin_fmv_deal_flag`.** New precomputed table `public.topshot_thin_fmv_editions` (edition_id PK, fmv_usd, median_90d, n_90d, computed_at; RLS on + anon/authenticated SELECT policy `USING(true)` — mirrors `topshot_conflated_editions` so anon can read it through the `security_invoker` deal view). New SECDEF fn `refresh_topshot_thin_fmv_editions()` (service_role only, statement_timeout 120s): cheap prefilter on the stored `sales_count_30d BETWEEN 1 AND 14` for HIGH/MED editions (→ ~3,276), then per-edition 90d median, flag where `n_90d<15 AND fmv>1.5×median`. **Measured ~4.5s / 96 flagged platform-wide** (≈10 currently intersect deals). `topshot_deals_vs_fmv` gains `low_confidence_fmv boolean` via a cheap LEFT JOIN (no other logic changed; conflation NOT-EXISTS + ORDER BY intact, security_invoker re-asserted). `cross_collection_deals_board` carries it through (TS leg = the flag; Pinnacle + AllDay legs = `false`, TS-scoped for now).
- **Migration 2 — `audit_20260621_dispatch_alerts_suppress_thin_fmv`.** `dispatch_due_deal_alerts(integer)` Pass-1 (edition-deal enqueue) gains one zero-cost line `AND NOT COALESCE(b.low_confidence_fmv, false)` — reads the already-materialized `tmp_deal_pool` column, so flagged editions never fire a fake "51% off" push. CREATE OR REPLACE preserved sig/SECDEF/search_path/90s timeout/grants (postgres+service_role). Pass-2 (per-serial board, different FMV basis) untouched. Verified end-to-end: 1 active sub, board built to 680 rows, ran with no error.
- **App (4 files).** `app/api/public/insights/deals/route.ts` + `app/insights/deals/page.tsx` select `low_confidence_fmv`; `DealsBoardClient.tsx` renders a muted amber `⚠ thin data — FMV uncertain` caveat (de-emphasizing the discount) on flagged rows + a methodology paragraph; `app/api/cron/refresh-conflated-editions/route.ts` calls `refresh_topshot_thin_fmv_editions()` as a **non-fatal sibling step** so the one (pending-operator) daily deal-board-guard cron keeps BOTH guards current — no new cron added.
- **Selectivity confirmed:** thin-variance offenders flagged; genuine high-discount deals on liquid editions are NOT (Jalen Green Throwdowns 52%, Draymond 46%, Jae Crowder 43% all `low_confidence_fmv=false`). Board ≥40% tail is now dominated by real deals.
- **Deeper lever (noted, NOT done):** anchoring FMV to the median (or a variance haircut) for low-sales editions would fix this everywhere FMV shows — a platform-wide pricing-philosophy change, deliberately separate (cross-ref the `dense-low-fmv-is-honest` principle: thin LOW is honest; this is the inverse, thin MEDIUM over-claiming).
- **Operator follow-up:** the `refresh-conflated-editions` daily cron is still unwired (1 run ever) — wiring it now also refreshes thin-FMV. Until then, both guard tables refresh when a session calls their RPCs (populated correct at ship via `SELECT refresh_topshot_thin_fmv_editions()`).
- **Verify:** `SELECT collection_slug, count(*) FILTER (WHERE low_confidence_fmv) FROM cross_collection_deals_board GROUP BY 1;` → TS 10. `curl -s 'https://www.rippackscity.com/api/public/insights/deals?collection=nba_top_shot&limit=200' | grep -c low_confidence_fmv`.
- **Revert.** Migration 2: re-`CREATE OR REPLACE` `dispatch_due_deal_alerts` without the `AND NOT COALESCE(b.low_confidence_fmv,false)` line (prior body in this session's transcript). Migration 1: `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` both views without `low_confidence_fmv` (TS leg drops the LEFT JOIN; board legs drop the column) → `DROP FUNCTION public.refresh_topshot_thin_fmv_editions();` → `DROP TABLE public.topshot_thin_fmv_editions;`. App: `git revert <commit>`.

### 2026-06-21 (daytime, Claude Code) — closed the LAST mis-attribution WRITER leak: `/api/ingest` + sales-indexer can no longer key TS sales/moments onto UUID-dupe editions (`6b9e89a`)
Drained `docs/handoff-2026-06-21-writer-leak-moments-feeder.md`. `f796447` fixed only the sales-indexer Step 4d GQL fallback, but **measured post-deploy the forward writer was still leaking** UUID-edition TS sales: ~30 `source='topshot_gql'` + 4 `source='onchain'` per 2h. Root-caused the 30 to the WRONG file vs the handoff — `source='topshot_gql'` is written by **`/api/ingest`** (the live GitHub-Actions cron, every 20min), NOT the sales-indexer (which tags `source='onchain'`). Both deployed forward writers now guarded. tsc clean; security 0 / secdef [] / trust 9/9 (no DB change). 1 commit (`6b9e89a`).
- **`/api/ingest` ([app/api/ingest/route.ts]) — dominant leak (30/2h).** When the hydrate-at-insert block can't map a UUID pair → int pair (genuinely-new / untracked plays that `searchEditions` can't surface), the UUID `editionKey` survived → `upsertEdition` created the inert UUID edition + `upsertSale` attributed the sale AND wrote a UUID-keyed `moments` row (the row that then poisons the sales-indexer feeder). Fix: a hard canonical guard in the per-tx loop — if the key is still a UUID pair after the UUID→int redirect, resolve `set:play` straight from chain via `getMintedMoment` (new `resolveIntPairFromChain`, per-run budget 60); if resolved use the int pair, else **`continue` (skip)** rather than write a UUID edition/sale/moment. New `ingest-canonical-guard` `pipeline_runs` telemetry (`uuid_resolved_onchain` / `uuid_skipped`).
- **sales-indexer ([app/api/sales-indexer/route.ts]) — 4 onchain leaks + feeder poison.** 4b (moments table) and 4c (wmc edition_key) trusted whatever edition the row pointed at, including UUID-dupe fossils, and the `stillUnresolved` filter then excluded those nfts from the fixed Step 4d resolver. Fix: `isCanonicalExtId` guard — 4b resolves each candidate moment's `edition_id → external_id` and keeps only canonical (`^[0-9]+:[0-9]+(::[0-9]+)?$`) rows; 4c only maps canonical edition_keys. Non-canonical candidates now fall through to Step 4d (getMintedMoment → int-pair → `ensure_topshot_edition_stub`); anything still unresolved is skipped (never a UUID write).
- **Refined the handoff premises (by direct read).** Item 2 (moments hydrator) needs NO change — `workers/topshot-moments-hydrator/index.ts` is already canonical-only (resolves editions strictly by `(set_id_onchain, play_id_onchain)` + `ensure_topshot_edition_stub`). Item 3 (sales-indexer Step 4d "no on-chain ids" branch, ~L475) is already clean — it logs and does nothing, no UUID fallback. The handoff's "8/23 moment-on-canonical" cases are point-in-time muddying from the daily drain re-keying moments/wmc AFTER ingest (they were UUID at ingest time).
- **Out of deployed scope: `source=NULL` / empty-txhash 2020 leak (56/3h).** NOT any deployed forward writer (ingest=topshot_gql, sales-indexer=onchain, history-backfill=ts_history_backfill_v1, all canonical-keyed). Signature (empty-string `transaction_hash`, no `source`, no moment row, 2020) matches a historical/local-node backfill path (`scripts/flow-backfill.ts` sets no `source` and `transaction_hash: sale.transactionId`) resolving editions off UUID fossils. Contained by the daily on-chain drain (its target set includes UUID-edition sales, getMintedMoment-resolvable for untracked). Operator/CC: don't re-run that script against the fossils until its edition resolution is canonical-guarded.
- **Verify (expect 0 on both).** `SELECT s.source, count(*) FROM sales s JOIN editions e ON e.id=s.edition_id WHERE s.collection_id='95f28a17-…' AND e.external_id !~ '^[0-9]+:[0-9]+(::[0-9]+)?$' AND s.ingested_at > now()-interval '2 hours' GROUP BY s.source;` → `topshot_gql` 0 and `onchain` 0. `ingest-canonical-guard` rows show `uuid_skipped`/`uuid_resolved_onchain`. Guard `topshot_conflated_editions` trends to 0 and HOLDS (currently 17, re-accruing from this leak pre-fix).
- **Revert.** `git revert 6b9e89a` (app-only; both writers fall back to prior behaviour). No DB change to revert.

### 2026-06-21 (daytime, Claude Code) — F1-F3 mis-key class RE-SCOPED + remediated: it is a platform-wide, ongoing TS sales mis-attribution bug, NOT 26 cheap commons (9,336 sales + 2,842 moments re-keyed to on-chain truth; self-healing remap wired; residual needs on-chain drain)
Investigated the "26-edition residual" (the prior entry's gated F1-F3 class). **The scoping doc's premise was wrong** — the 27 guard editions were the only fraction the 365-day same-serial detector can see of a broad, still-active mis-attribution: `sales.edition_id` is wrong for a meaningful fraction of TS sales (the moment truly belongs to a different play, usually same player, true edition mostly Series-1 Base). **Proven by 9 direct on-chain `TopShot.Collection.borrowMoment` reads** — every one matched `wmc.edition_key` and contradicted the recorded sale edition. Blast radius among held moments alone: ≥4,020 canonical mis-attributions; 1,398 collision groups across 119 editions once true editions were consolidated. The live `onchain` source was 5.37% wrong, `topshot_gql` 68.7% wrong. Root cause: `app/api/sales-indexer/route.ts` resolves edition once at ingest via `wmc→moments→GQL` and never re-resolves, inheriting corruption from the pre-2026-05-26 wmc-canonicalize era, the canonically-corrupt `moments` feeder (~1,200), and a GQL fallback that maps GQL UUIDs onto inert UUID-dupe editions. Full write-up: `docs/scoping-2026-06-20-26-edition-misattribution.md` (rewritten). security 0/[] not affected (read + data re-key only).
- **`audit_20260621_remap_misattributed_topshot_sales`** — re-keyed **9,336 sales** to canonical on-chain truth (edition + true serial), only where the moment's wmc edition_key is unambiguous int-form (incl `::subID`). Before-state: `audit_topshot_sale_misattrib_remap_20260621`. **Revert:** `UPDATE sales s SET edition_id=b.old_edition_id, serial_number=b.old_serial FROM audit_topshot_sale_misattrib_remap_20260621 b WHERE s.id=b.sale_id;`
- **`audit_20260621_revert_ambiguous_wmc_sale_remaps`** — wmc itself is ~3% corrupt (10,280 groups map 2+ moments to the same edition+serial); reverted the **106** re-keys that landed on such ambiguous truth rather than guess. Captured in `audit_topshot_sale_remap_reverted_ambiguous_20260621` (flagged for the on-chain drain).
- **`audit_20260621_remap_misattributed_topshot_moments_safe`** — re-keyed **2,842 moments** to wmc truth, only where the target `(edition,serial)` slot is free (`moments` has `UNIQUE(edition_id,serial_number)`). Colliding twins deferred to the drain. Before-state: `audit_topshot_moment_misattrib_remap_20260621`. **Revert:** analogous UPDATE from the audit table.
- **`audit_20260621_remap_misattributed_topshot_sales_fn`** + **`audit_20260621_guard_refresh_runs_misattrib_remap`** — durable self-healing `remap_misattributed_topshot_sales()` (idempotent, ambiguity-guarded, service_role-only) now runs inside `refresh_topshot_conflated_editions()` before the guard computes, so the tracked set keeps converging every cron tick (and re-corrects future ingest-time mis-attributions for tracked moments). **Revert:** restore the prior two function bodies (in the migrations).
- **Residual (needs the on-chain drain — environment-gated):** 113 editions / 1,389 collision groups / ~2,567 colliding nfts held by UNTRACKED wallets (no DB on-chain truth). Guard honestly shows 44 (365d) and suppresses them from deal boards. **Open follow-ups:** (1) build the prod `getMintedMoment`-via-topshot-proxy drain (Stage-B pattern) to resolve sold-on/ambiguous nfts and zero the residual; (2) writer fix in `sales-indexer`/hydrator (resolve through on-chain truth + fix the GQL UUID→edition mapping + re-resolve on wmc arrival); (3) `fmv-recalc` the ~2.4-4k affected source+target editions (cron sweep covers them; force-stale accelerates).

WHERE external_id IN (<16>) AND created_at >= '2026-06-20'`.

### 2026-06-20 (daytime, Claude Code) — historical buyer-backfill lane tuned (skip unreachable 2022 + raise batch)
Two-constant tune of the LIVE `?mode=historical` spork lane in [app/api/admin/backfill-topshot-buyers/route.ts](app/api/admin/backfill-topshot-buyers/route.ts) (pipeline `topshot-buyer-backfill-historical`). Baseline null-buyer rows (verified): 2022=17,123 (unrecoverable, now excluded), 2023=19,604, 2024=7,817 (~27.4K recoverable).

Verified the guard exists before raising.

### 2026-06-20 (daytime, Claude Code) — AllDay listing-serial backfill moved ON-CHAIN (nflallday consumer GQL is Cloudflare-1009-blocked)
Drained Item 1 of `docs/handoff-2026-06-20-allday-1009-and-drain-cadence.md`. The `allday-listing-serial-backfill` pipeline had failed **every** run since creation (2026-06-19) with `http_403: error code: 1009` — and the handoff's premise (sibling `allday-unmapped-resolver` "runs clean 72/0" → it's a request-fingerprint/UA difference fixable in the edge fn) was **disproven by direct measurement**: the resolver gets 0 targets every run (its AllDay backlog drained), so it never exercises the consumer endpoint; its consumer-GQL leg has **never** returned rows in retained history and last logged `consumer_gql_error` 2026-06-16. The deployed backfill request was already byte-identical to the resolver, so the `f53a92118` UA "fix" couldn't have worked. Root cause: **nflallday's Cloudflare returns 1009 (region/IP ban) to the topshot-proxy worker egress on the `/allday-consumer` route** — an upstream block, not a header difference (the worker already injects browser Origin/Referer/UA). public-api.nflallday.com (`/allday`) is unaffected (allday-fmv-populate / -sales-indexer / -listing-cache all 72/0). Fix moves the serial source **off nflallday entirely**: borrow each floor moment on-chain from its seller via the AllDay-typed `borrowMomentNFT` (the exact script the healthy allday-sales-indexer uses), against `rest-mainnet.onflow.org` (proven reachable from Supabase edge fns).
- **Migration `allday_listing_serial_targets_add_seller_address` (MCP-applied, CC).** DROP+CREATE `get_allday_listing_serial_targets(int,bool,int)` → return shape `TABLE(nft_id text, seller_address text)` (was `nft_id` only), joining `cached_listings_v2` on the floor `listing_resource_id` to carry the seller. Only the edge fn calls it (grep-verified). SECDEF + `search_path=public` + grants (postgres/anon/authenticated/service_role) preserved. **Revert:** `DROP FUNCTION public.get_allday_listing_serial_targets(integer,boolean,integer);` then re-CREATE the prior `RETURNS TABLE(nft_id text)` body.
- **Edge fn `backfill-allday-listing-serials` v3 (MCP-deployed, verify_jwt=false).** Replaced the consumer-GQL chunk loop with a bounded-concurrency (8) on-chain borrow pool over the targets: `getAccount(seller).capabilities.borrow<&AllDay.Collection>(/public/AllDayNFTCollection).borrowMomentNFT(id)` → `{serialNumber, editionID}`, 2-retry on transient Flow REST faults, 130s soft budget. nil borrow (moment moved/sold = stale listing) is silently skipped (no error). `ok` is true unless every target faulted. **Verified live:** the 03:34Z cron run flipped to `ok=true / targets=170 / resolved=37 / upserted=37 / borrow_errors=0` (vs `ok=false / http_403:1009` for every prior run); `allday_moment_serials` 2→39; `cross_collection_deals_board` AllDay leg now carries `low_ask_serial` on 38/171 deals. ~69% of the freshest board listings resolve on-chain; the overall unresolved are **stale floor listings** (V2-fork-dormant indexer missed their ListingCompleted → moment already left the seller's account) — a pre-existing deal-board data-quality issue, NOT a serial-backfill flaw. `check_public_security_invariants()`=0, `check_secdef_anon_execute_violations()`=`[]`. **Revert:** redeploy the v2 source / prior consumer-GQL index.ts.
- **NOTE — stale AllDay floor listings (queued, deeper).** The borrow nils surfaced that `allday_edition_floor_ask` carries ~130 `source='direct'` floor listings (mostly $1–$7, May–June) whose moments have moved — the dormant V2 Flowty fork indexer isn't marking `completed_at`. The deal board shows these as buyable floors. Separate from serials; worth an operator/CC pass to expire un-held `direct` AllDay listings.
- **Item 2 (sales-history-backfill cron cadence) — NOT done, by design.** Converting `topshot-sales-history-backfill` to 202+after() to move it to cron-job.org would reintroduce the silent-after()-tail/telemetry-loss failure the route was explicitly engineered to avoid (synchronous + ~120s self-budget; see its header + [[vercel-after-finally-unreliable]]), and yields nothing without a separate operator cron-job.org entry. Left as a recommendation. Queue: 8,426/9,091 pending (draining via GHA ~15/day).

Added per-page `HARD_CAP_MS=265s` deadline guards inside `resolveSetPlayMap` + `ingestEditionSales` so no single edition can run toward the 300s gateway cap (the historical 180s-budget→300s-kill was an unbounded set-map). With set-map de-risked: `ELAPSED_BUDGET_MS` 180s→240s, `EDITIONS_PER_TICK` 80→120. **Cadence (cron frequency) remains the operator-side lever** to drain the ~8.5K queue proportionally faster. **Revert:** `git revert 02705a5`.
- **Item 1 — inert spork historical buyer lane (`03062c2`).** The forward `topshot-buyer-backfill` decodes via current mainnet REST, which only serves current-spork txs (~late-2024+), so it can't resolve the historical null-buyer tail. **Measured null-buyer breakdown: 2020–21 ~137K (65%), 2022–24 ~42K (20%), 2025–26 ~30K (14%, already current-spork → the existing forward lane drains these — no spork needed).** Built but SHIPPED FULLY INERT: (a) `workers/spork-proxy/index.ts` gained a `?tx=<hex>[&spork]` passthrough that WALKS mainnet19→26 newest→oldest to find a tx by id (no block_height needed — we don't store it; a tx in none is pre-mainnet19 → `tx_not_found_in_listed_sporks`); (b) `decodeTopShotSaleTxViaSpork` reuses the shared `parseTopShotSaleTxJson` over the worker; (c) `POST /api/admin/backfill-topshot-buyers?mode=historical` — window [2022,2025), own pipeline_runs row `topshot-buyer-backfill-historical`, conservative `HIST_BATCH=40` + the same 600s wall-bound. **OFF unless `TS_HISTORICAL_BUYER_BACKFILL_ENABLED=1` AND `SPORK_PROXY_URL`/`SPORK_PROXY_SECRET` are set.** OPERATOR to enable: `wrangler deploy` spork-proxy → verify one known 2022 tx decodes through the worker → set the Vercel env vars + flag → wire a low-cadence cron. **The 2020–21 bulk (~137K) is pre-mainnet19 and NOT recoverable via the wired sporks — a separate, larger effort (would need mainnet1–18 nodes).** **Revert:** `git revert 03062c2` + `wrangler deploy` the prior spork-proxy.
- **Item 4 — dapper.market venue: CONFIRMED already indexed, no change.** Recent decoded onchain TS sales (11,852/7d, 1,063 distinct buyers) all carry the single custodial payer `0x18eb4ee6b3c026d2` — the Dapper `NFTStorefrontV2` escrow/router. dapper.market and the native marketplace both settle through `A.4eb8a10cb9f87357.NFTStorefrontV2.ListingCompleted`, which the sales-indexer already watches (alongside `TopShotMarketV3.MomentPurchased`). No venue gap; the execution-account monitor would surface a new payer if a new venue appeared.
- **Item 5 — multi-factor special-serial FMV model: DEFERRED (data-gated).** Confirmed the binding constraint: only **1,122 serial-#1 sales (909 editions) + 627 perfect-serial sales** platform-wide — far too sparse to fit a player×badge×set×series×team pooled hedonic model (full spec in strategy §6). **Trigger to build:** Items 1–2 drain for a few weeks and the special-serial sample materially rises; then fit offline (statsmodels MixedLM / sklearn Ridge), write a coefficient table, apply in SQL at read-time with per-prediction confidence, keep the current power-law as fallback. Ships as reviewed FMV pricing logic, not a blind edit.

### 2026-06-18 (daytime, Claude Code) — drained the audit follow-ups handoff (Items 1–4 + Cowork mojibake migration logged)
Drained `docs/handoff-2026-06-18-audit-followups.md` end-to-end (the 06-18 full-platform-audit follow-ups). 2 code commits + 2 DB migrations; tsc-clean (PowerShell, 0 lines on changed files). None touch FMV/pricing/auth/secrets.
- **Item 1 — buyer-backfill BATCH 200→150** (`app/api/admin/backfill-topshot-buyers/route.ts`). The after() drain does one ~2.9s on-chain decode per row, so BATCH=200 ran ~577s against `maxDuration=600` (~23s headroom; observed 539–577s/run last 24h) — creeping toward the silent lambda-kill ceiling where the run dies BEFORE the finally writes its pipeline_runs row (invisible-failure class). 150 → ~435s, comfortable headroom. Throughput stays fine (150/run × ~10 runs/day ≫ ~270/day new-null inflow; the 208k null backlog is a low-priority historical tail). `maxDuration` left at 600. **Revert:** set `BATCH` back to 200.
- **Item 2 — `dispatch_due_deal_alerts` 0-subscription early-exit** (migration `audit_20260618_dispatch_due_deal_alerts_zero_sub_early_exit`). Even at 0 active subs the fn still materialized BOTH deal-board temp tables every run (`cross_collection_deals_board` + `topshot_underpriced_serials_board`), and that build alone exceeds the 45s `statement_timeout` under load → alerts-dispatch logging "deal: canceling statement due to statement timeout" ~3×/24h. Added a guard right after BEGIN: `IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM alert_subscriptions WHERE active=true)` → return a zeroed summary with `skipped:'no_active_subscriptions'`. Purely additive (passes through when subs exist; byte-identical behavior). CREATE OR REPLACE, same signature, grants preserved. Verified live: 0 subs → returns instantly with `skipped`; `check_secdef_anon_execute_violations()`=[] (the count(*)=1 is the fn's single empty-array row, not a violation); `check_public_security_invariants()`=0. **Scale path when subs go live:** bump fn `statement_timeout` 45s→90s (route maxDuration must cover) OR refresh the two boards into a cached table on a separate cron. **Revert:** CREATE OR REPLACE the prior body without the guard (pre-migration `pg_get_functiondef` in this session's transcript).
- **Item 3 — `normalize_pinnacle_edition()` de-double-encode hardening** (migration `audit_20260618_normalize_pinnacle_edition_de_double_encode`). The BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger now self-heals byte-level double-encoded UTF-8 on set_name/character_name/franchise: `replace(chr(226)||chr(128)||chr(162) → chr(8226) [•])` and `(chr(226)||chr(132)||chr(162) → chr(8482) [™])`; franchise de-encodes first so the existing ™-strip still applies. Verified: firing the trigger on the 2 residual rows the Cowork one-time fix missed (set-level rollup rows, `external_id` null) cleaned them to a proper `•` → board-wide mojibake count now **0**. Any future write self-heals even if the upstream writer regresses. **Revert:** CREATE OR REPLACE `normalize_pinnacle_edition()` without the de-double-encode pass (prior body: TRIM-only on the 4 columns + the franchise ™-strip).
- **Item 4 — `/insights/underpriced-serials` staleness caption** (`app/insights/underpriced-serials/UnderpricedSerialsBoardClient.tsx`). The "Updated …" line tracks page-render time (always near-now); the real listings freshness is `max(last_seen_at)` across rows (Atlas curl-ingest spine, ~3h residential-runner cadence that can skip overnight). Added a `listingsAgeHours` memo + a muted `--rpc-warning` caption "Listings last refreshed Nh ago" shown only when the spine is ≥4h old — honest when the runner lags, invisible when fresh. **Operator (not done — infra):** confirm/repair the Atlas residential runner's overnight schedule. **Revert:** `git revert` the client commit.
- **Cowork (logged, not CC) — migration `audit_20260618_pinnacle_editions_fix_double_encoded_mojibake`:** one-time UPDATE that de-mojibake'd existing `pinnacle_editions` display strings (Cowork shipped live this session; Item 3 above is the durability follow-up that keeps it from reappearing on re-ingest). **Revert:** none needed (idempotent data correction); the corrupted form is recoverable only by re-ingesting the bad upstream string, which Item 3 now blocks.

### 2026-06-18 (daytime, Claude Code) — alert line "parallel type" ask: tier+mint + formal parallel/variant + parallel filter (`0933067`/`11c6c1a`/`2ac4bcf`/`a6c3afd` + 2 migrations)
Drained the two 06-18 alerts handoffs end-to-end (Trevor: "include the parallel type"). All 4 deploys READY (prod www). Key learning: Top Shot's FORMAL parallel (`badge_editions.parallel_name`) is base/Standard on 99.97% of editions (only Galactic ×2 + Diced ×1 catalog-wide), so the SET NAME is the meaningful TS variant and the real gaps were tier+mint (TS) and the Pinnacle variant. **Verified each step against live deal-board data — which caught two Pinnacle correctness bugs before users saw them.**
- **Part 1 — tier + mint display (`0933067`).** `lib/alerts/format.ts` `dealSubline` now renders `#1 · Rare · Metallic Gold LE · /222 · NBA Top Shot` — added `dealTier` (title-case) + `dealMint` (`/<circulation_count>`). Both fields already rode the deal payload from both dispatcher passes; the formatter just wasn't surfacing them. No type change needed (`tier`/`circulation_count` already on `DealPayload["deal"]`). Flows to all 3 channels. **Revert:** restore the prior `dealSubline` body.
- **Part 2 — surface formal parallel/variant (`11c6c1a`, migration `dispatch_due_deal_alerts_surface_parallel_variant`).** Added a `parallel` key to both passes of `dispatch_due_deal_alerts`: TS (by `external_id`) = `badge_editions.parallel_name` excluding `''`/`Standard`; Pinnacle (Pass 1, by `render_id`) = `pinnacle_catalog.variant` excluding `Standard` (`parallel_type` is null platform-wide). `dealSubline` renders it after the set name; `parallel: string|null` added to the Deal type. CREATE OR REPLACE (grants preserved). **Revert:** re-CREATE the prior function body (no `parallel` key) + drop the `dealParallel` segment.
- **Part 2 fix (`2ac4bcf`) — caught in end-to-end verify.** The cross-board `tier` column for **Pinnacle holds the variant itself** (Colored Enamel / Golden / …), not an NBA rarity enum. So (a) part-1's `dealTier` mangled "Colored Enamel" → "Colored enamel" — fixed to only title-case all-upper enum values; (b) `dealParallel` would double-print the variant — now skips when it equals `tier` (case-insensitive); TS Galactic/Diced differ from RARE/LEGENDARY and still render. **Revert:** restore the prior `dealTier`/`dealParallel`.
- **Item 3 — parallel/variant filter (`a6c3afd`, migration `alert_subscriptions_parallel_names_filter`).** Adds `alert_subscriptions.parallel_names text[]` (nullable = no filter) + a WHERE clause to both passes of `dispatch_due_deal_alerts` AND the `build_deal_alerts_for_subscription` preview (so preview count matches dispatch). Match value mirrors the displayed parallel: TS named parallel from `badge_editions`, Pinnacle variant from the board `tier`. A null resolved value never matches → setting the filter narrows out base editions (expected). API: subscriptions sanitizer accepts `parallel_names`; UI: `/alerts` chip multi-select (15 Pinnacle variants + Galactic/Diced) wired through FormState/payload/editSub. Verified live: a `Golden`/`Color Splash` filter matches only those Pinnacle variants, TS base editions excluded. CREATE OR REPLACE (grants preserved). **Revert:** drop the column + re-CREATE both prior function bodies + drop the UI/API field.
- **Full revert:** `git revert a6c3afd 2ac4bcf 11c6c1a 0933067` + the 2 migration reverts above. Handoffs: docs/handoff-2026-06-18-alert-display-tier-mint.md, docs/handoff-2026-06-18-alert-parallel-surface-QUEUED.md (both marked SHIPPED).

### 2026-06-17 (daytime, Claude Code) — per-moment jersey-match (`967a1b2`)
Drained `docs/handoff-2026-06-17-jersey-match-per-moment.md`. Jersey-match was derived from `players.jersey_number` (one last-known value per player) → a number-changer matched his CURRENT number on every moment, not the number worn in that play. Top Shot's jersey-match is per-MOMENT.
- **Handoff correction:** the GQL field is `play.stats.jerseyNumber` — already fetched by ingest, just mis-stored to `players` (last-write-wins). And the handoff's suggested `searchEditions` backfill **won't work** (TS GQL rejects integer set/play IDs; our canonical editions are integer-keyed). Jersey is a property of the PLAY, available on-chain via `TopShot.getPlayMetaData(playID)["JerseyNumber"]` — verified on-chain (Trae Young play 523 → "11", Harden play 1437 → "13").
- **SHIPPED — migration `audit_20260617_editions_jersey_number_per_moment`:** `editions.jersey_number smallint` (nullable) + service-role-only `backfill_edition_jersey(jsonb)` (UPDATE by `play_id_onchain`, gated to TS collection_id, search_path set). **Revert:** `DROP FUNCTION public.backfill_edition_jersey(jsonb); ALTER TABLE public.editions DROP COLUMN jersey_number;`

### 2026-07-08 (Cowork interactive) — concierge check_wallet told the truth-free version of a $82k portfolio; fixed end-to-end (eeff0b1 + 187669e, verified live)

Trevor's first real Telegram DM QA exposed three data bugs in the concierge wallet path (bot reported "24 moments / $146.77" for a wallet actually holding **18,796 moments / ~$82.4k**; "UFC" answers mirrored Top Shot; AllDay errored with $0). All fixed + live-verified via the trusted-bot smoke path (`tg:cowork-smoke-4`: full correct rundown, one tool call; smoke rows flagged).

- **`eeff0b1` — wallet-search unknown-slug fall-through (the UFC-mirrors-TS bug):** any unrecognized collectionId (e.g. `ufc-strike`, which the model naturally invents) silently fell through to the Top Shot on-chain walk, returning TS moments labeled as the other collection. Now: aliases normalized (underscores/bare names/ufc-strike) and unknown slugs 400 with the valid list. Applies to ALL callers of /api/wallet-search.
- **`eeff0b1` — check_wallet reads `get_wallet_collection_snapshot` first** (the /share RPC; 222ms warm): full-wallet totals + per-collection breakdown + top-5 + rarest + badge count across all 5 collections in ONE call — replaces the old path that walked the chain and reported a single 24-row page (limit default) as the portfolio, and enriched AllDay ids through TS GQL (guaranteed `Unknown (error loading)` + $0). Optional collectionId adds that collection's wmc top-5. Unindexed wallets fall back to the live TS walk with `summary.totalMoments` + an explicit page-only note. Tool schema now enumerates the exact 5 slugs and instructs ONE call for cross-collection questions.
- **`187669e` — per-tool timeout budget:** the blanket 6s Promise.race killed check_wallet twice at exactly 6000ms during the known contention window (trace 04:27Z) even though the snapshot RPC is 222ms warm. Wallet tools (`check_wallet`, `check_wallet_squeeze`) now get 20s; all other tools keep 6s. This was also the cause of Trevor's very first "Wallet lookup timed out" DM on the OLD code (12s inner fetch under a 6s race = guaranteed loss under load).
- **Revert:** `git revert 187669e eeff0b1` (code-only, no migrations). Smoke rows: `DELETE FROM support_conversations WHERE session_id LIKE 'tg:cowork-smoke-%';`
- **Note for the night pass:** wallet-search's AllDay leg still enriches via TS GQL/Cadence internally (pre-existing; concierge no longer depends on it for indexed wallets). If a real web consumer hits it, the durable fix is wmc-based assembly for the AllDay branch — CC lane, not auto-shippable.

### 2026-07-07 (Cowork interactive) — conversational concierge over Telegram + Discord DM (d07ed2f + a30c790, deploys READY; /ask registered)

Trevor: make the bot concierge conversational ("white glove — meet users where they are"), not command-only. Diagnosed + shipped end-to-end; smoke-verified live on prod (session `tg:cowork-smoke-2`: identity "I've got you, jamesdillonbond" + memory recall across turns; rows flagged is_smoke_test).

- **Root finds:** (1) bot DMs had NO memory — support-chat only replays client-passed `conversationHistory` (web widget); the bridge never sends it. (2) **Regression:** the cookie-only `deriveIdentity()` hardening silently killed the 06-17 `b00b914` DM personalization — the bridge's body `ownerKey` was dropped, every bot DM answered anonymous. (3) Discord had no conversational path at all (Interactions webhook = slash-commands only). (4) Telegram replies showed raw markdown, no typing indicator.
- **support-chat (`d07ed2f` + `a30c790`):** `isTrustedBotRequest` — `x-rpc-bot-secret` header, timing-safe vs INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN **or CRON_SECRET** (a30c790; the two are treated as equivalent everywhere else — the INGEST-only first cut no-op'd for CRON-keyed callers, caught by live smoke). When trusted + `pageContext='bot_dm'`: accepts bridge-resolved `ownerKey` (+ allow_list wallet lookup) and rebuilds the last 8 turns from `support_conversations` by session_id (`loadBotDmHistory`). New bot_dm system-prompt branch: plain text, chat-length, bare URLs, conversational follow-ups.
- **concierge-bridge:** sends the trusted-bot header (INGEST).
- **Telegram:** sendChatAction typing indicator; markdown→plain-text sanitizer (`toTelegramPlain`); 4096-char split; concierge-aware /help; maxDuration 60.
- **Discord:** new `/ask question:` slash command → concierge (deferred reply, `dc:<userId>` session, linked-handle personalization, 2000-char cap); maxDuration 60. **Registered globally** via POST /api/bots/discord/register → `["link","soldpacks","alerts","ask"]` (global propagation to DMs ≤ ~1h).
- **OPERATOR (Trevor):** confirm `ALERTS_BOT_CONCIERGE=1` is set in Vercel prod env — it gates BOTH the Telegram free-text branch and `/ask` (not readable from Cowork; everything else is verified live). Also: `.env.production`'s INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN (May 12) is STALE — it now matches live CRON_SECRET, not INGEST; worth refreshing the local file on next rotation pass.
